The sight of Shaolin, who seemed ready to jump into the water and come to their rescue, hesitantly retreating and then forming a line again, was clearly visible.

“What....”

What were they doing?

Why were they stopping there? What on earth were they planning to do?

“Why...?”

Someone was clearly heard mumbling that.

Silence settled in.

A terrible silence descended on the island, making it hard to believe that just a moment ago, the mad beasts were biting and running wild.

“Why... why are they not coming?”

“Just...”

Until then, the Namgung family’s men still held onto a faint glimmer of hope. There must be a reason. It was a little delayed, but Shaolin would surely come across the river to support them.

They believed it.

No, there was no other way for them than to believe that.

Even Namgung Hwang had no doubt that Shaolin would cross the river to save them at that moment.

But...

“W-what is that ship?”

A single word that came out of someone’s mouth spread like ripples.

The gaze that Shaolin had suddenly taken away turned to the side. Only then did everyone’s eyes catch the sight of the fleet approaching the island.

At that moment, everyone there understood what was happening. It was a judgment based on sense and not reason.

The Namgung family’s eyes shook greatly. Filled with distrust and fear, they slowly turned their gaze back to the other side of the river. They trembled pitifully as if they couldn’t muster up the courage to confirm the reality they were facing.

However, the moment when they must face the truth would surely come.

When their anxious gazes finally turned back to Shaolin, they saw it. Shaolin was standing on the far-off riverside.

What made those who had seen the despair of the Namgung family was not the tip of Shaolin’s goals, as they took a step back like they had no intention of coming anymore.

What truly made them despair was the absence of their eyes.

The eyes of the Shaolin monks, who should have been staring at the island with sharp focus, weren’t visible.

Because they were not looking there.

They were called the great Shaolin of the world, the Big Dipper of the Murim world. Those very arhat monks of Shaolin, who never bowed their heads to any sect in the world, were now bowing their heads like sinners.

As if they couldn’t bear to look at each other.

Even with this much distance between them, they were turning away from the gaze of the Namgung family as if their guilt was too great to bear.

How could they not know?

Even an idiot would know what was happening there.

They were... abandoned.

“Ugh....”

“Uh....”

The thing that pushed people into the greatest despair was always hope.

Humans did not despair where there was no hope at all. They took that for granted.

But humans who found a sliver of hope would try to cling to it.

And when they realized that hope was nothing more than a mirage, humans finally crumbled.

“Uh... uh...”

An indescribable despair began to engulf the Namgung family.

The fact that they had been abandoned by their trusted allies, that Shaolin had turned a blind eye to their deaths, plunged them into a bottomless pit of despair they had never experienced before in their lives.

“Ughh....”

A groan, like a wounded animal panting, escaped their lips.

Maybe it wasn’t all that different after all. In reality, they were now wounded animals, and all that was left was death. With the lifeline called Shaolin cut off, all that was left was an endless fall.

They no longer had the will or the spirit to swing their swords. Their limbs felt heavy, as if they belonged to someone else. They just wanted to let everything go.

Frustration is scary because it makes people collapse. Everyone was being helplessly sucked into a deep abyss that was too dark to even dare to look into.

However, at that moment, only one person let out anger instead of despair.

“ABBOOTTTTTTT!”

A desperate scream burst out from the mouth of the wounded tiger, Namgung Hwang.

“Abbot! ABBOOTTTTTT! EUAHHHHHHH!”

A blood-boiling howl burst from his throat.

The blood vessels in both eyes burst out, and the rested throat let out this roar like twisted iron.

Just then, when everyone was groaning under such desperate frustration.

He finally arrived on the land, the one who considered all this muddy despair as mere fun.

Kuaaang!

The large battleship crashed into the sand.

Namgung and the pirates, even Namgung Hwang and the Black Dragon King, watched the scene.

“Eumm.”

A low nasal voice flowed out lightly. It was a very small sound, but it strangely spread clearly throughout the island.

Finally, a person appeared with light steps.

“....”

Namgung Hwang stared at the figure with red eyes, his blood vessels bursting.

It was the figure of the one who had ridiculed them in this very river three years ago.

That person hadn’t changed at all.

A gorgeous red robe with strange embroidery engraved with gold thread. Pure white skin, hair tied up so that not a single strand fell, a gorgeous crown, and even excessive ornaments.

Tak.

Jang Ilso, who lightly jumped from the ship to land, slowly straightened his slightly bent body.

Clank.

The sound of jewelry he wore clashing against each other forcibly brought back memories from three years ago. That sound awakened nightmares and brought in new fear.

Namgung Hwang’s gaze fell on Jang Ilso’s lips.

“So strange.”

Jang Ilso spoke softly with an extremely friendly face.

“It has been a while, but you don’t seem to be welcoming at all.”

His lips, red as blood, drew a curve like the moon. The words pouring out from those lips felt truly ominous and terrifying.

Namgung Hwang tightly shut his eyes.

“I am... glad to see you here. Hahahaha!”

And then he thought.

It might have been better to die at the hands of the Black Dragon King.

Instead of being toyed with by this devil....

He might not be the strongest.

Jang Ilso was called the best in the world, but no one knew whether his strength was truly the best. No, no one even wanted to know that.

The reason? It was quite simple.

That wasn’t even that important.

The position of everyone living in Kangho was determined by their strength. To build fame in the dangerous situations of Kangho, one had to prove their strength repeatedly.

But this guy... wasn’t the only one.

Jang Ilso, the Hegemony Lord.

That name was the only one that stood out in this Kangho.

Even if Jang Ilso’s strength was no more than that of a third-rate backstreet thug, everyone in the world would say it in unison.

This guy... was the most dangerous person in the current Kangho.

The viper carrying a deadly poison slowly walked across the sandy beach. His steps were light rather than threatening and elegant rather than serious. It was a leisurely gait as if he had come out for a walk.

But no one on Plum Blossom Island could take their eyes off that sight.

“...”

Dry saliva rolled down his face.

No one had ordered them to stop fighting. But the war naturally stopped.

At least Namgung Myung seemed to know why.

No rabbit would fight for territory in front of a wolf.

The moment Jang Ilso appeared, everyone here instinctively felt that now wasn’t the time to fight one another.

“Uhm.”

A low, nasal voice blew through the wind across Plum Blossom Island.

“This...”

Jang Ilso’s gaze turned to Namgung Hwang.

“It looks like it isn’t worth it...”

Namgung Hwang clenched his fists. It felt like a cool, scaly snake was strangling his neck.

In fact, this wasn’t just a feeling. Namgung Hwang’s eyes could see the ship that followed Jang Ilso, without docking at the island, leisurely turning its direction and blocking the river between Plum Blossom Island and Shaolin.

Now, even if Shaolin belatedly decided to come and help, it would be difficult to break through.

Unlike his peaceful steps, Jang Ilso was definitely squeezing their breath.

As if to confirm this, the warriors disembarked from the Ten Thousand People Clan and ran, following Jang Ilso. Ho Ga-Myung ran out and stood next to him as if guarding him.

Even though he knew this wasn’t the situation to laugh, Namgung Hwang felt like he would end up laughing in vain.

Jang Ilso and a guard? Could there be words that didn’t go well together?

At that moment, the corners of Jang Ilso’s mouth rose strangely.

“The Namgung family of the azure heavens... how did it end up like this? Uh?”

The snake-like gaze choked the breathing of the exhausted Namgung family’s swordsmen. Jang Ilso took a deep breath and laughed.

This was fun.

He liked this sight so much that it gave him goosebumps.

The Blue Sky’s Namgung Family.

Weren’t the eyes of those who shone with such splendid fame now filled with void and despair?

Some were so resentful they were ready to cry, and some were devastated by the fact that it was all over. Some were glaring at him with eyes full of venom, and others were looking at him with eyes that yearned for some mercy.

All of these different eyes were focused on Jang Ilso.

However, there was one thing in common among those eyes.

There was no more light of hope to be found.

Step. Step.

Jang Ilso stepped ahead.

The pirates blocking the Namgung family’s path moved to the left and right with expressions of fear. A surprisingly wide path opened up in a place where there was no room to step because the pirates were densely packed.

Jang Ilso walked along the path leisurely, as if it were natural.

The footsteps finally stopped in front of the Namgung family’s Blue Sky Sword troops. These were the ones guarding the battle line. They were the best swordsmen that the Namgung family boasted about.

They fought a short but fierce battle. As a result, the Blue Sky Sword troops who were still standing were covered in blood, cuts, and stab wounds.

As Jang Ilso approached, they instinctively raised their swords and aimed at him, baring their teeth like cornered beasts.

However, Jang Ilso continued to approach them with the same slow steps, as if he couldn’t even see the swords pointed at him.

Flinch.

Then, the tips of the swords of the Blue Sky Sword troops began to tremble.

It was a truly bizarre and eerie situation where the ones threatening with swords were trembling, while the one being threatened, with bare hands, was laughing.

“D-don’t...”

The member standing right in front of Jang Ilso barely managed to hold out a trembling sword. He threatened Jang Ilso by extending the tip of his sword further as if he would pierce his neck at any moment.

But Jang Ilso seemed to know that this sword would never pierce his neck... or perhaps he didn’t care even if his throat were cut.

When he finally stopped walking, the tip of the bloody sword was almost touching his neck.

All one had to do was stab it.

By channeling qi and pushing his arm forward, he could pierce that pale white neck. All he needed was a bit of courage.

But...

The sword never advanced any further. It trembled so pitifully that it was hard to believe it was held by a swordsman of the Namgung family. In fact, he backed away, even more intimidated.

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso smiled seductively and slowly reached out, placing his fingers on the trembling blade.

Swish.

At that moment, they saw.

Jang Ilso’s fingertips split slightly, and a drop of red blood flowed down the sword.

“....”

Before seeing this sight, everyone vaguely thought that such a hand would not be hurt by a common sword. That was why he felt so terrified of them.

But the moment they saw that drop of blood, their hearts clenched even harder.

Those who did not bleed and therefore did not fear death, and those who bled but did not fear death.

Which one was scarier?

Swish.

The white fingers adorned with ornaments slowly advanced along the pathetic, trembling blade. Gently caressing the bloody tip, they finally reached the hand of the swordsman, who was tightly gripping the handle of the sword.

Jang Ilso’s fingers, which climbed slowly and sharply up the arm like a snake, finally brushed the Blue Sky Sword troop member’s shoulder, stained his pale cheek with blood, and reached his forehead.

It was just a light and slow movement, but everyone could do nothing more than stare at the sight. It was as if they were possessed.

At that moment.

Jang Ilso’s hand, which had been spread out, suddenly opened.

At that moment, everyone flinched at the sudden change.

Tak.

Jang Ilso’s large hand covered the head of the Blue Sky Sword troop member as if he were stroking the head of a small child.

“...pity.”

Jang Ilso’s affectionate voice, filled with compassion, softly resonated.

“You all fought so hard....”

The Blue Sky Sword troops trembled all over at that. The Namgung family swordsmen, who were watching, bit their lips until they bled.

How should they describe this situation where they received comfort from the enemy and were momentarily comforted by that comfort?

When Jang Ilso appeared there, did even a single person expect such a thing to happen?

Jang Ilso slowly shook his head. Then he opened his mouth again.

“And yet in the end...”

He blurted out the words that the Namgung family members absolutely didn’t want to hear.

“You have been abandoned.”

At that moment, everyone felt breathless.

They had seen despair and then hope again and fell from the hill of hope to the pit of hell. Those were the words they absolutely didn’t want to hear.

“Such a poor thing... tsk tsk.”

Jang Ilso’s eyebrows lowered as if he truly felt pity.

Namgung Hwang gritted his teeth at the abominable sight. He could no longer bear to watch.

“Jang... Ilso!”

Jang Ilso slowly turned around, his expression filled with bewilderment as if he had forgotten Namgung Hwang was there.

“... do not mock us.”

“Mock?”

Jang Ilso repeated as if he had heard something amusing and then laughed.

“Right. Mock! A warrior would rather die than be mocked! It would be better to have a clean kill!”

Jang Ilso smiled at Namgung Hwang’s words.

“It is strange, you know... the more I hear it, the stranger it gets.”

“... what is so strange about that?”

“When did I ever make fun of you?”

“...”

His innocent expression seemed genuine, though everyone knew it was an act. But that wasn’t what mattered. That one expression, that one gesture, showed how much he could unsettle them.

“The one who had fun with you... wasn’t it Shaolin?”

“What kind of nonsense is that!”

“Am I wrong?”

Jang Ilso raised his hand, pointing to the sky before slowly lowering it to indicate the Shaolin across the river.

“See.”

“....”

“It is just a river. There is no reason for them not to cross it. Wouldn’t the name of Shaolin cry if they couldn’t cross the river with simple ships blocking the way?”

Namgung Hwang bit his lip.

“But what are they doing? They are simply watching. They are just observing your final moments as you die here.”

“Jang Ilso!”

“And that is...”

Jang Ilso lowered his hand, a blatant sneer forming on his lips.

“That is what mocking means.”

Namgung Hwang clenched his fists so hard that the skin around his bones turned white.

Jang Ilso continued.

“They came running as if they were going to save you as if they were some great saviors, but in the end, they have no intention of shedding blood to save you. That must be what they think your worth is.”

Namgung Hwang knew he wasn’t angry because he was being mocked.

The man’s words were not wrong at all, which was why they were so painful to him. A scream that he couldn’t bring himself to shout formed in his throat.

“... So? Are you trying to mock us who ended up in this situation?”

He gritted his teeth and spoke.

“Don’t make me laugh, Jang Ilso! The Namgung family will not be a joke to anyone! If death is meant to happen, I will fight until the last moment! Every one of us will! The fact that we were abandoned will not tarnish the name of the Namgung family!”

He spoke so carelessly that he didn’t even understand what he was saying.

But the anger in his voice seemed to have been clearly conveyed to those who used the name of Namgung. The eyes of those who were losing hope began to light up.

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso shook his head as if he was having a hard time.

“Come ahead, Jang Ilso! I will be the first to die!”

Namgung Hwang gathered all his strength.

He must be the first to show the Namgung warriors, who were trembling in fear, that a warrior proves himself through death.

Jang Ilso just stared blankly at Namgung Hwang, who was raising the spirits as if he would rush to fight at any moment.

At the very moment when Namgung Hwang was about to rush in, holding his sword, Jang Ilso slowly opened his mouth.

“... Should I do it then?”

Namgung Hwang’s body stopped. It was like he had been struck by lightning.

Disbelief, shock, frustration, and anticipation.

All those emotions surged through Namgung Hwang’s eyes like waves in a short moment.

“What... now...”

That was the devil’s whisper. He should never have heard it. But Namgung Hwang had no choice but to hear it.

“What... did you say?”

Jang Ilso smiled broadly. He was like the devil who crawled out of hell and spoke to a despicable human.

With red lips, he blurted out the same words from before in the same tone.

“Should I save you then?”

“....”

A deep and heavy silence descended on Plum Blossom Island.

“Hahahaha....”

And in the midst of that silence.

The devil laughed out.

“AHHAHAHAHAHAHAH! EHAHAHAHAHAHAH!”

Jang Ilso’s laughter and the jingling of the jewelry filled the temporary residents’ ears on Plum Blossom Island.

Who didn’t know?

This was nothing but a joke. The words coming out of that devil’s mouth were nothing more than dragging them down to the ground, trampling on them, mocking them, and laughing at them.

But... was there anyone who didn’t waver?

The moment the word “save” came out of Jang Ilso’s mouth, was there even a single person who didn’t harbor even a little bit of hope and longing in their hearts?

Namgung Do-Wei closed his eyes tightly.

‘I...’

The moment he heard Jang Ilso’s words, even Namgung Do-Wei, who was prepared to die, felt a strong impulse. The impulse to live. The fear of not wanting to die.

If there weren’t people watching him around, he might have knelt before Jang Ilso and held onto his legs.

Begging for his life. Not wanting to die. Not yet... death wasn’t right yet.

Who would be different from that?

No matter how upright you tried to stand, how could your desire to live be different? Jang Ilso’s one word easily dug up that desire that was hidden deep inside.

“This...”

Namgung Hwang clenched his teeth, unable to say anything.

He must have felt the same way as Namgung Do-Wei right then. The trembling of his hand wasn’t in anger at Jang Ilso but anger at himself for not being righteous at that moment.

In the end, Namgung Hwang shouted in a seething voice.

“You evil bastard!”

Jang Ilso’s eyes looked at him oddly.

“This time, evil.”

“...”

“Hmm. Well... that is what I like to say. Evil, mean, cowardly... well, whatever. Nice. But....”

Jang Ilso slowly shook his head.

“Not in this situation. Namgung Hwang. Now is not the time to curse at me.”

Namgung Hwang went silent. He had intended to briefly understand Jang Ilso’s real thoughts, but it wasn’t enough. With his skills, he could never see into that monster’s true feelings.

“It isn’t something to think only of as bad.”

Jang Ilso gently turned his body and slowly approached Namgung Hwang. Namgung Hwang’s sword still hadn’t let down its guard, but Jang Ilso remained calm and relaxed.

Jang Ilso leaned in and whispered softly in Namgung Hwang’s ear.

“Now... Namgung Hwang. Think about this, okay? You have to think desperately now. Being abandoned isn’t necessarily a bad thing, right? You are free now. Isn’t that true?”

“....”

Namgung Hwang bit his lip and glared at Jang Ilso. His blood-stained face, disheveled hair, and bloodshot eyes were nothing short of brutal.

But did Namgung Hwang know?

What did it mean for a swordsman, who must maintain his stance, to lose himself to words? That this action alone proved beyond doubt that he was wavering.

“You people....”

He spat out.

“Are you telling me to be your subordinate? Do you think the Blue Sky Namgung family will be the dogs of the Evil Sect?”

He shouted so that everyone shaken by the devil’s voice could hear it clearly.

“If we die, we die, but we will not be your dogs! Do not make me laugh now, Jang Ilso!”

Everyone agreed with that statement. Survival is important for everyone. However, there is clearly a value that cannot be exchanged for the sake of survival.

The Blue Sky Namgung family.

They could accept death, but they could never become subordinates to Jang Ilso. If they could, they would not have thrown away peace and invaded Plum Blossom Island.

At that moment, when strong antipathy towards Jang Ilso was about to engulf everyone.

“... dogs?”

Jang Ilso’s calm yet puzzled gesture, as he slightly tilted his head, forcibly drew everyone’s attention.

“Again... you speak of strange things which I don’t understand.”

Jang Ilso raised his eyebrows and exaggeratedly laughed at Namgung Hwang.

“Ga-Myung.”

“Yes, Alliance lord.”

“Did I say that to them?”

“You said nothing, lord.”

Ho Ga-Myung answered right away as if he didn’t have to think about it. His face, in stark contrast to Jang Ilso’s, was so expressionless that it was impossible to guess what he was thinking.

“Then why do they say such things?”

“Because everyone thinks within their own limitations, lord. It is only natural that they cannot keep up with the Alliance Lord’s thoughts. It isn’t something we can blame them for.”

“Hmm, right. It makes sense.”

Jang Ilso nodded leisurely and looked at Namgung Hwang with a big smile.

Namgung Hwang could only watch the conversation, which was like an opera.

From the moment Jang Ilso arrived there, everyone seemed to be moving according to the roles Jang Ilso had set up. Even breathing in and out, getting angry, and expressing frustrations.

“No, that isn’t it, Namgung Hwang. And...”

Jang Ilso’s gaze turned from Namgung Hwang to the Namgung family swordsmen.

“Namgung family.”

It seemed to tell him that the authority to decide this matter wasn’t just Namgung Hwang’s but also the family’s swordsmen.

“Why do you think I am deceiving you?”

“....”

“Because I am from the Evil Sect?”

“...”

“I am just telling the truth.”

They were drawn in.

To that voice. That tone. With that one gesture.

“I will tell you again. That is not what I want. You don’t have to be a dog, and you don’t have to join the Four Precepts Alliance. There is just one thing I want.”

His thin lips twisted.

“Just let the words’ please save me’ come out of your mouths. Right. That will do.”

Everyone stared at him, lost in thought.

Not only the Namgung family but also the pirates were the same. Their eyes were filled with bewilderment as they looked at him.

The faces of many people filling this island were dyed in the same color; it was truly a miracle.

Were they in their right mind? Is the man really saying such things now? Why? Just why? Why!?

Jang Ilso stood there among those eyes filled with disbelief and shock, giggling.

“It isn’t so hard, right? Is it?”

“....”

“That is fine. I will spare you the moment you say it. I will just simply let you go. Right there. To the north of the river, where you so desperately wanted to return. To the comfortable and cozy place where your home is.”

The devil’s whisper must have a deadly poison that they could not even guess.

But... even if they knew this fact, nothing would change.

How could they ignore such sweet words?

“I will even let you collect the bodies and take them away. Even though they died here, they deserve to be buried in their homeland, right? Right? Because they fought harder than anyone else.”

The Namgung family’s swordsmen turned their gaze, unable to overcome their instincts. The sight of their family and comrades dying without even having the chance to close their eyes was etched into their minds.

“It isn’t difficult.”

Jang Ilso said as if soothing a child.

“Just say one word. Just say one word, and everything will come true. In the name of mine, Jang Ilso, the Hegemony Lord....”

He paused for a moment.

Everyone waited for his words with thirst.

“... I promise.”

After the sweet and fatal words came, there was a brief silence.

And then someone spoke as if possessed.

“Save m....”

“NO!”

At that moment, Namgung Do-Wei, who was close by, reached out and covered the man’s mouth. Then he shouted with an angry face.

“Jang Ilso!”

Jang Ilso frowned slightly.

“Now, now. How dare such a great warrior close the mouth of someone who wants to live, only to send them to their death. How shameful, even the Evil Sect doesn’t do something like this.”

“Shut up, you fucking moron! What nonsense are you even talking about!”

“Hmm?”

“Don’t make fun of the Namgung family! We will not fall for your....”

At that moment, Jang Ilso’s eyes, which had appeared gentle until then, contorted immediately. The presence was so surprisingly fierce that Namgung Do-Wei unconsciously stopped talking and took a step back.

The pale eyes were as fierce as if blue flames were rising around them.

“You think I am a fool or something?”

Jang Ilso clenched his teeth.

“I will tell you, young lord. It isn’t me who is being a fool; it is you.”

“...”

“Shaolin has abandoned you. No, the Justice faction has abandoned you. I will ask now. Do you think any of the sects that followed Shaolin across the river would be willing to shed blood for you?”

No one could readily answer that question.

Of course, he wanted to shout. He wanted to yell and curse at them not to look down on the Alliance of the sects.

But hadn’t he already seen how the Shaolin sect, which protected the factions of the Central Plains, had abandoned them?

“Your lives are so insignificant to them. But... no, not them either. It is you who consider your lives as even more insignificant.”

“....”

“Life isn’t like that.”

Keeik.

Jang Ilso’s rings rubbed against one another, making an eerie sound.

“The value of life is something you decide for yourself. At least, that is how I am. I stabbed a knife into the stomach of someone I called a comrade, bit the neck of someone who called himself a friend, rolled around in filth, and bit the cliff with my teeth when my arms broke!”

“....”

“That is what it means to survive and live.”

Everyone knew that these words were neither persuasion nor threats. This was Jang Ilso’s true nature.

“But... you are telling me that I am being ridiculous here?”

Jang Ilso bared his teeth.

“If you don’t make fun of those who choose death because they couldn’t utter a single word to be saved, who else in the world can ridicule your actions? Why should I treat those who consider their worth lower than insects any better? Answer me, boy! What right do you have to decide the death of someone whose mouth you silenced?”

Namgung Do-Wei’s hands trembled.

If Jang Ilso had spoken one more word, Namgung Do-Wei might have collapsed right there.

But Jang Ilso relaxed his force at that moment. He slumped his shoulders as if exhausted.

“It isn’t difficult.”

“.....”

“No one remembers your death. The glory gained through death is nothing more than crumbs that the survivors throw to the dead. Useless crumbs taken from enormous loot.”

Someone gulped, and someone clenched their fists at that.

“It is so sad to die holding onto those crumbs... isn’t it?”

He almost nodded.

At those whispered words.

“So... tell me.”

Jang Ilso’s eyes, filled with momentum, took on that bright and mysterious smile again.

“Fight for your life. That will work. That will be enough for you to survive.”

Step. Step. Step.

Jang Ilso walked towards the Blue Sky Sword troops. He whispered in their ears, those who couldn’t even think of using their blades.

Softly but clearly and slowly so that everyone could hear it.

“Do you want to live?”

Those who heard that voice froze in place.

“You want to live. Everyone wants to live. But those who try to use their lives sparingly say it easily. A warrior must know how to throw away his life for pride. He must discard life like a rag toy for the sake of his side.”

Both eyes trembled. Jang Ilso recited the question that came to the minds of the swordsmen whose hearts were trembling.

“For what?”

“....”

“No, no, you will know when you live. Pride isn’t as great as you think. It is like the night that passes when you close your eyes for a moment. So... tell me, I want to live. I want to survive.”

“I-I....”

In the end, tears flowed from the eyes of the Blue Sky Sword troops.

A swordsman who had been trained through countless years of harsh trials, a member of the Namgung family, who was not afraid of death and was prepared to protect the name of Blue Sky with all his life, was crying like a child.

It was an unbearable pressure.

A glimmer of hope followed by great despair.

At the end of that, hope, will, and harmony were powerless in front of the one who grabbed their souls and twisted them.

“Rest now. Right?”

Red lips couldn’t resist the words.

“Say it now, with that mouth.”

The eyes of the swordsman, who didn’t know where to go, cried out pitifully with a hoarse voice.

His hands, trembling and unable to find their way, finally grabbed Jang Ilso’s sleeve.

“Save me....”

“Stop now, Hegemony Lord.”

At that moment, Namgung Hwang intervened.

His voice was weak as if it wasn’t his at all.

“... you have enjoyed it enough.”

It felt as if his soul had left him as if he had aged several years at once.

Namgung Hwang’s head slowly dropped.

“We....”

He spoke in a voice that showed no sign of pressure.

“We... lost.”

It was the moment when everything for Namgung Hwang had collapsed.

The abbot gazed at Plum Blossom Island with a bewildered expression.

The fleet of the Ten Thousand People clan, led by Jang Ilso, obstructed their view of the island. Still, it wasn’t difficult to see between the ships and assess the situation.

‘...What is happening?’

The war was in full swing. Jang Ilso had arrived at Plum Blossom Island amidst such a fierce situation. Even a three-year-old child could guess what would happen next.

But nothing was happening on the island. Not a single scream or clash of weapons could be heard.

It was quiet.

The excessive silence seemed to pierce the heart in an eerie way. The corners of the abbot’s lips trembled.

He could tell intuitively what the source of that silence was.

Jang Ilso, the Hegemony Lord.

It was clear that he was plotting something immediately. Now, it seemed as if a giant snake that threatened even the sky was wrapping itself around the island.

But...

‘What is it?’

What on earth could one attempt in such a situation? The moment Jang Ilso arrived, he... No, Shaolin set foot on the ground, the conclusion was almost a foregone conclusion.

“...Amitabha.”

A murmur escaped his lips.

He couldn’t read it at all. All he could sense was Jang Ilso’s sincere malice emanating from the island. Just looking at it from afar gave him goosebumps...

‘Please...’

Whatever Jang Ilso was after, if he got involved, the end was obvious. The abbot hoped that the Namgung family would take the right action.

However... the abbot didn’t know it at the time.

What he really should have been paying attention to wasn’t the Namgung family’s reaction but what the snake’s fangs wrapped around the island were truly after.

Jang Ilso’s mouth twisted strangely as he looked back at Namgung Hwang.

Namgung Hwang looked like a person whose soul had been snatched away. The appearance that had once embodied overwhelming power was nowhere to be found. All that remained was the look of a defeated man who had been deceived by a three-inch tongue and had denied everything about himself.

“Hmmm.”

Jang Ilso slowly licked his red lips with his tongue and made a sound.

“I lost....”

“....”

“No, Namgung Hwang.”

Namgung Hwang slowly raised his head, which had been bowed down. His chin, trembling slightly, seemed to betray his struggle to hold in his emotions.

“You should ask for your life to be spared, right?”

“....”

“Cowardly and miserable. Right, that is what you need to say.”

That wasn’t the kind of tone one would use with an enemy. It was as if he were lightly scolding a disobedient child. Perhaps that tone was several times more humiliating to Namgung Hwang than the situation itself.

“You seem to be misunderstanding something. This isn’t a match between you and me. It’s definitely not a match between the Namgung family and the Ten Thousand People Clan. Do you get it?”

Jang Ilso’s big smile was so bright that it seemed almost cruel to witness.

“You don’t even have the right to talk about losing to me.”

Namgung Hwang’s shoulders trembled.

The giant who had carried the name of Namgung was collapsing, infinitely miserably and cruelly.

“So speak again properly.”

Jang Ilso’s whisper spread clearly throughout the island, where everyone was holding their breath.

“Please spare us.”

“....”

“Come on, try and beg like that, Namgung Hwang. That is how you become a good child.”

Jang Ilso’s face was filled with the look of vicious victory. The expression alone spoke of the situation more clearly than words.

Jang Ilso literally pushed Namgung Hwang and the Namgung family into a terrible sense of defeat without lifting a finger. Even if they had all died fighting, they wouldn’t have felt such a terrible sense of defeat.

Namgung Hwang took his eyes off Jang Ilso and looked at his family members.

These were the people who came here solely because they trusted him, never once complaining, even as things became dire. Did he have the strength to witness their deaths with his own eyes?

Namgung Hwang’s mouth twisted open, and a cry more desperate than a scream tore out.

“SPA....”

Jang Ilso stared at him with a smile.

“SPA...RE....”

His jaw trembled, his eyes were red, and his hands shook like thin branches.

It was the moment when the giant of the era was finally about to put everything down.

Paaaat!

Suddenly, a sword flew in and slammed into Jang Ilso’s face in one swift motion.

“Hmm?”

Kakang!

Jang Ilso calmly raised his hand, blocking the sword with the ring on his fingers.

For a moment, dead silence fell.

The most astonished were the Namgung family members. They all opened their eyes wide and gasped in shock at the situation.

They groaned in despair at the thought that their lives, which had barely been secured, might be cut off with that one attack.

Kakak!

The sound of Jang Ilso’s ring twisting the blade rang out eerily.

“...What are you doing, young lord?”

Namgung Do-Wei.

He gritted his teeth, stabbed Jang Ilso with his sword, and growled as he put strength into the hand that held the sword.

“Do not make me laugh, Jang Ilso.”

“....”

“I will die here.”

Jang Ilso looked at him with respect.

“D-Do-Wei!”

A loud scream burst out from Namgung Myung’s mouth.

“What are you doing! Go back!”

“And what if I back off!”

“W-what?”

Namgung Do-Wei glanced back, gritting his teeth. His red eyes looked as if they would pierce Namgung Myung at any moment.

“If I back off, will you bet your life on a snake like this and return to Anhui?”

“....”

“And you will tell the children in the family mansion that you fought the Evil Sect to the very best and returned?”

Those last words were close to a scream.

“Father!”

Namgung Do-Wei shouted at Namgung Hwang.

“A swordsman who has lost his will is not a swordsman. What I must protect until the end is not my life but the soul of a warrior!”

Namgung Hwang’s body trembled.

“You taught me that! You, of all people! And now you are telling me to beg for my life from the Evil Sect!?”

Namgung Hwang bit his lips tightly at that desperate cry and said,

“... S-step back, Do-Wei.”

“NO! I will not step back.”

“Do-Wei!”

Namgung Do-Wei glared at Jang Ilso.

“Did you say that one’s life is one’s own choice, Jang Ilso?”

“Yes.”

“Then I...”

Namgung Do-Wei’s eyes were wavering; it wasn’t a word he spoke with firm determination and conviction. In fact, even as he spoke, his heart was unsure several times.

But that is why those words held more power.

“I will die here.”

“...”

The moment those words fell, all of Namgung’s swordsmen trembled.

A young swordsman? An innocent choice from someone who didn’t know the world?

That cannot be true.

Because those trembling hands were telling them. Right now, Namgung Do-Wei was fighting the fear of death head-on. He gritted his teeth and stood up to the great evil called Jang Ilso.

“Namgung Do-Wei! What...”

Namgung Myung desperately tried to stop him, but Namgung Do-Wei cut him off.

“You can live.”

“...”

“Go back alive too. That isn’t wrong. It is the right choice.”

Namgung Myung fell silent.

“But at least one person... at least one person must protect the will of the Namgung family. For the sake of the kids in the Namgung family who will face the people who return!”

Namgung Do-Wei’s lips were bleeding.

“That’s the role of the one who carries the blood of the Namgung family.”

No one could retort at that.

What needed protection until the end wasn’t life itself but the soul of a warrior. This was something Namgung Do-Wei and the others had heard countless times. Yet, they were driven to the brink and ultimately failed to uphold their moral code.

In that dire situation, only Namgung Do-Wei, still considered young, was safeguarding what he had learned.

A sense of solemnity and shame engulfed them simultaneously, leaving them troubled.

Some raised their heads with red eyes, some lowered theirs in shame, and others clenched their fists so tightly that their nails dug into their skin.

The reactions varied, but their thoughts were clear. The candle, now extinguished and emitting only smoke, held a faint spark.

Perhaps it was nothing.

The arrogance, the agreement—it could all be an illusion, as Jang Ilso had said. But there was someone present who would risk his life for such a cause.

Clench.

Someone was gritting their teeth. The heightened emotions spread like wildfire from their feet, and their bloodshot eyes followed Namgung Do-Wei’s form.

The one they had been protecting.

The future of the Namgung family, which they had vowed to protect all their lives.

That future was now sacrificing itself to preserve the last pride of the family. If they were to call themselves warriors, if they knew how to wield a sword, they could not witness this without feeling moved.

The venom in their dead eyes began to stir once more. It seemed as if they could erupt again if someone opened the gate.

But at that moment, they heard the voice of Jang Ilso, with its languid nasal tone.

“Uhahaha.”

And as the following words reached their ears, everyone in the Namgung family instinctively sensed something was amiss.

“Right.”

Even Namgung Do-Wei looked at Jang Ilso in shock.

The Hegemony Lord gazed at Namgung Do-Wei with a gentle smile.

“Right, that is it, Namgung family. I was worried. I was worried that you might beg for your lives and end up going back alive.”

Namgung Do-Wei thought.

This guy was a devil who came to deceive the humans.

That voice, the soft and warm voice, with no ill intent, is the devil’s voice. Behind that voice, there is something dark and heavy that humans cannot even dare imagine.

“What a wonderful young lord.”

Jang Ilso smiled brightly. It seemed like he was praising a child who had done a good thing.

Clack,

Jang Ilso pushed away the sword in his hand and lightly shook his head.

“There is nothing I can do.”

Then he slightly spread his arms and slowly shrugged his shoulders.

Then he slightly spread his arms and slowly shrugged his shoulders.

“If you would not accept my mercy, then I have no choice but to do what it is that you want.”

“....”

“Ga-Myung.”

“Yes, Alliance Lord.”

“Withdraw everyone.”

Ho Ga-Myung looked at the Alliance Lord with wide eyes of shock. The confusion and shock were evident in his eyes, even to the Namgung family people.

“... are you talking about the Ten Thousand People clan?”

Normally, he would never have asked Jang Ilso anything, but Ho Ga-Myung was quite shocked.

Jang Ilso clicked his tongue with a slightly annoyed expression.

“Ga-Myung, you don’t understand what I am saying. I clearly said, everyone. The entire Four Precepts Alliance will withdraw from the island.”

“A-Alliance Lord. That...”

Then Jang Ilso glanced at Ho Ga-Myung. Ho Ga-Myung flinched at the cold gaze and lowered his head.

“I will carry out the orders.”

“Yes, yes.”

As if nothing had happened, Jang Ilso smiled again and slowly glanced at Namgung Hwang, the Namgung family, and Namgung Do-Wei.

“What a touching moment.”

And he nodded like an actor on a drama stage, with an expression of pure admiration.

At that moment, Namgung Do-Wei’s heart was filled with even more anxiety than before.

“Then... it would be polite to give him a fitting end.”

Jang Ilso turned around, his red long coat fluttering in the strong, wind-like wings.

A quiet and calm voice flowed from him as he turned.

“Die here while agonizing over whether your choice was right.”

He had no regrets now that he had made the decision.

Jang Ilso walked away, staggering ahead. Then Ho Ga-Myung gestured with his hand.

The pirates, who had been shocked by the sudden situation, seemed to freeze in place after receiving the order but soon began to leave Plum Blossom Island in confusion.

It was an absurd sight—the pirates who had held swords to the necks, lowering them without killing their opponents. The Namgung family couldn’t even react; they just watched.

“This is...”

Namgung Myung mumbled in a low voice.

The eyes of the Namgung family swordsmen began to grow suspicious.

Maybe...

Was Jang Ilso’s proposal really not mercy, then?

Was he not driving them away with some hidden intention but really trying to let them be?

That couldn’t be it. It absolutely cannot be it...

Then why did the man turn around like that without even a hint of foolishness? Why didn’t he force them into anything? No, why didn’t he take their lives himself?

What on earth had he done now?

In a situation where no one could even guess how to accept this, only Namgung Do-Wei had one thought.

Maybe everything that happened on this island was Jang Ilso’s plan.

From the moment Jang Ilso first arrived here until this moment, maybe they had never escaped the palm of Jang Ilso for even a moment. That horrible thought.

“Alliance Lord. Those people...”

“Surround the island with ships so that not a single ant can escape.”

“Uh? Yes! I will do so!”

Ho Ga-Myung bit his lip, then cautiously asked.

“What are your plans for the future? May I ask?”

Jang Ilso smiled brightly.

“What can I do? I have to show them what true despair is.”

“...Ah.”

Ho Ga-Myung nodded as if he understood.

“To them, a comfortable death is a luxury.”

“Tsk tsk, Ga-Myung. Why are you so unresponsive today? That’s not like you. And it isn’t that.”

“...Uh?”

When Ho Ga-Myung asked incredulously, Jang Ilso smiled, twisting the corners of his lips.

“Namgung was never on my radar from the start. My goal was always...”

His eyes slowly moved.

“Those central plains bastards.”

Jang Ilso’s eyes, looking at Shaolin across the river, were filled with a sense of dread.

“The plan has finally been set. It is just the start. Come now, let us show them how many horrible sides are inside me!”

As he watched Jang Ilso laughing as if in awe, Ho Ga-Myung felt goosebumps running down his body.

It was the moment when Jang Ilso exposed his fangs to the world.

The Black Dragon King gritted his teeth. The pirates were retreating from Plum Blossom Island.

“This....”

Clench!

He clenched his lips and trembled with anger.

‘Those idiots.’

He had never given any instructions to the bandits. His orders had stopped at annihilating the Namgung family. Yet, those morons were retreating according to Ho Ga-Myung’s orders.

“This...”

Of course, Ho Ga-Myung was Jang Ilso’s right-hand man, the leader of the Four Precepts Alliance. Looking at the chain of command, it wouldn’t be wrong for them to follow his orders. After all, the pirates were also members of the Four Precepts Alliance.

However, seeing the pirates follow Ho Ga-Myung’s orders without even checking, despite him being right there, made his stomach churn.

“Phew.”

The Black Dragon King took a deep breath.

Anger surged, but if he raised his voice, he would have to admit that they had taken away his command of the pirate fleet, even if only for a moment. Instead, he gritted his teeth and jumped off the Black Dragon Ship.

He then approached Jang Ilso, who was heading to the ship.

“Jang Ilso!”

He called out to him in an angry, beast-like voice.

“What are you doing? Why are you letting them live like this? If I just twist their necks now, it will all be over!”

Jang Ilso’s feet stopped. He didn’t even turn his body but only turned his head to look at the Black Dragon King.

“Tell me something...”

The words that had been urging him were cut off. The Black Dragon King unconsciously bit the flesh inside his mouth.

Jang Ilso’s gaze showed not a single trace of emotion.

The moment his colorless, odorless gaze pierced through him, the Black Dragon King felt a chill as if he had fallen into an ice cave.

“Uhh....”

Jang Ilso remained silent and stared at the Black Dragon King. The Black Dragon King tried to meet his gaze, which was so transparent, with red eyes, as if he had to prove who he was at that moment.

But the standoff did not last long. In the end, the Black Dragon King turned away first.

“Excuse me, Alliance Lord... I got too excited.”

A slightly bowed head.

It was not a gesture of complete submission, but it showed sufficient respect for the other person.

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso looked at him with a slightly displeased expression and slowly nodded.

“I know why the Black Dragon King is angry. He must be upset because I arbitrarily took the pirates.”

“Ah, not necessarily....”

“But it isn’t something to be so angry about.”

Jang Ilso smiled broadly and continued.

“Killing them and getting rid of them isn’t such a huge deal, is it?”

The Black Dragon King doubted his ears for a moment.

‘Not a big deal?’

They were the Namgung family, the leaders of the Five Great Families, and the prestigious families of Murim, which had existed for hundreds of years. Just wiping them out would be an achievement that the Four Precepts Alliance and the pirates would be proud of for eternity.

But... that wasn’t a huge deal!

“Even so, it is a loach.”

Jang Ilso.

“If you catch a loach, you should use that loach as bait to catch something like a snakehead. What difference would it make if you catch just one loach?”

“... a snakehead?”

“That is the huge fish.”

Jang Ilso slowly turned his head. His gaze stopped at the Black Dragon Ship, the symbol of the pirates. A strange smile appeared on his lips.

“I will give you a more detailed explanation when I get on board the ship.”

The Black Dragon King’s expression hardened slightly. But the moment Jang Ilso turned around as if to ask if there was a problem, the Black Dragon King had no choice but to nod.

“... I get it.”

“Ga-Myung, let’s go.”

“Yes!”

Jang Ilso and Ho Ga-Myung leisurely headed towards the Black Dragon Ship. The Black Dragon King followed them silently.

“....”

He quietly bit his lips and glared at Jang Ilso’s back as he walked ahead.

The Black Dragon Ship symbolized the Black Dragon King’s authority. No one could board the ship without the Black Dragon King’s permission. Jang Ilso could not have been unaware of that fact, but now Jang Ilso had informed the Black Dragon King that he would board the Black Dragon Ship.

Yes, it wasn’t a request or permission but a notice.

This clearly showed the positions of the Black Dragon King and Jang Ilso.

‘Jang Ilso...’

The Black Dragon King clenched his fist.

But now he couldn’t stop him. If he had tried to stop him, he shouldn’t have let go of the initiative the moment they first confronted each other. The moment he couldn’t handle the gaze and avoided it, the Black Dragon King lost the right to control him.

‘How...’

Three years ago.

When they first formed the Four Precepts Alliance, Jang Ilso wasn’t like this. The reason the Shenzhou Five Sects took Jang Ilso as the Alliance lord was because they were confident he could match the abbot of Shaolin.

But now, the question that had taken root kept coming up.

Was it really possible to control that snake?

As he watched Jang Ilso boarding the ship with his red robe fluttering, cold sweat began to form on his back.

The face of the abbot who was watching the Plum Blossom Island turned blank.

The pirates who had filled the Plum Blossom Island retreated one by one and boarded the ship.

“This...”

The abbot, who had been mumbling something, closed his mouth.

He tried to say, ‘How on earth was this going to turn out?’ but he couldn’t bring himself to say it. He knew how foolish those words would sound to the ears of those guarding behind him.

However, his worries were nothing more than an old man’s concerns. Even if he had said those words, no one would have pointed a finger at him or called him foolish.

They also did not understand the situation. No, even if someone from the world came, they would not understand what was happening right before their eyes.

“... it seems like they were stepping down.”

Those were the words that came out of the mouth of the abbot. However, they were not words spoken to the abbot. The abbot was also in a state of confusion, mumbling what it was.

“Namgung... was already under attack?”

“... as if.”

No matter how surrounded they were, the Namgung family was the Namgung family.

In particular, wasn’t there the Emperor’s Sword, Namgung Hwang, over there?

No matter how powerful Jang Ilso or the Black Dragon King were, it would be impossible to subdue that Emperor’s Sword without much fuss.

Then...

‘What on earth is going on over there?’

Why wasn’t there a battle then?

And why didn’t the place descend into a battle, but the pirates who cornered the Namgung just walked away?

Did they just let go of the cornered enemy out of mercy?

How ridiculous.

If they were like that, they wouldn’t have been called the Four Precepts Alliance in the first place.

“Amitabha... Amitabha.”

The abbot quickly chanted.

He couldn’t figure out what was happening. From the moment Jang Ilso appeared there, his head felt like it was going to be messed up.

“Abbot, then...”

The abbot turned his head and looked at Beop Gye.

“Namgung.... And them...”

“Can’t you keep your mouth shut!”

At that moment, an angry roar burst out of the abbot’s mouth.

There was no way the abbot didn’t know what Beop Gye was trying to say. He probably wanted to suggest that Namgung had joined hands with them. However, he should never have said that out loud.

“Don’t talk about things that haven’t been confirmed yet! It is insulting to them!”

“I am sorry, abbot. But the situation is a bit...”

Although he was angry, he understood the abbot’s feelings for saying those words.

No, to be honest, the abbot had already considered that possibility. He thought it was impossible, but if it wasn’t, then it was an incomprehensible situation.

“Amitabha. Amitabha.”

The abbot bit his lip. Just then, he saw someone wearing a red robe boarding the Black Dragon Ship.

‘What are you even thinking!’

There was no need to think about who was in charge of this whole thing.

Jang Ilso.

It was clear that the evil man had done something.

But even the abbot couldn’t figure out that man’s true intentions.

Crack.

It was the moment when the abbot glared at Jang Ilso, gritting his teeth.

“T-they are moving.”

“...”

The ships that had been anchored to the island slowly retreated. Even the men who had pushed ships that were stuck on the sand climbed back onto the ship.

“T-they are really leaving like that?”

“... why on earth?”

“What are they thinking?”

A mumble came from behind.

The monks Shaolin, which was considered the strictest sect in the world, were so confused that they were talking about things right in front of the abbot.

“Are they really retreating from the island?”

“... what is that...”

They could see it.

The island where all the pirates had retreated and Namgung’s survivors standing around.

Beop Gye spoke again with a pale face.

“A-abbot. This means that they did make a deal...”

“Did I not tell you to shut up!”

Beop Gye went silent as anger was poured onto him. But the abbot clenched his fists.

‘It was such an obvious trick.’

The Namgung family made a deal with them and remained alive?

How could that have happened?

The abbot was not one to suspect and reject the Namgung family for something like that. What could they not do to live? A gentleman would not hesitate to crawl between the legs of the enemy and even lick his balls in order to achieve a cause.

There was no reason for the abbot to get caught up in such an obvious trick.

“That cannot be. It is something else. But just what is... what is it that can be gained from this situation?”

But the abbot soon realized.

It might have been better not to know.

“The ship...”

“A-Abbot. The ships are surrounding the island again.”

The Shaolin monks shouted in bewilderment. As expected, the ships that had left the island didn’t completely retreat but began to slowly surround Plum Blossom Island.

“Surround?”

Again, really?

If that was the case, why not kill...

“Uh...”

At that moment, the abbot’s eyes widened as if they would fall out.

“Euk!”

In an instant, a sound like the soul escaping from the mouth burst out. It was a groan that Beop Gye, who had been by the abbot’s side, had never heard.

His mouth was open wide, and his eyes trembled in fear.

“H-how...?”

The ships that had thickly surrounded the island clearly turned outside the island, towards the north of the river.

“H-How can a person do this? How can a human do something like this? How! Just how!”

A desperate scream burst from the abbot’s mouth.

“A-Abbot!”

Beop Gye, who still didn’t understand the situation, was shocked by this. He could promise that this was the first time the abbot’s anger was this violent.

“Hegemony Lord! Hegemony, Lord, you wicked man of hell! How can a human even do this? Just how does a human do this?”

“A-Abbot! Please calm down!”

That was the moment.

The massive and intimidating Black Dragon Ship slowly approached them, cutting through the water. Soon, the ship reached the river between Shaolin and Plum Blossom Island and began to slow down as it halted.

No one could breathe.

No one could open their mouths.

A man in a red robe stood leisurely on the sharp bow of the Black Dragon Ship.

No one could move a finger; they just stared at the man, Hegemony Lord, Jang Ilso. Every expression on his face, every gesture, every corner of his robe fluttering in the wind—everything was captivating.

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso’s eyes were fixed on Shaolin.

“Why are you so angry?”

A soft, gentle voice pierced the silence.

“I am...”

The pause was brief, but the silence made everyone’s hearts sink. Soon, the devil smiled and continued.

“I came to give you a chance.”

His blood-red lips curved like a crescent moon.

The word “chance” and Jang Ilso didn’t go well together. The man was more like a devil who took away the chances of others, played with them, and then cast them into a deep abyss.

But who could remain unmoved by the word “chance”?

Those in front of him had no choice but to listen to Jang Ilso.

The gusting wind lightly tossed Jang Ilso’s neatly arranged hair. His pure white fingers gently brushed the flowing strands.

His mouth slowly began to open again.

“Isn’t it... really touching?”

Jang Ilso’s gaze slowly turned to the back. Though it was clear that the high-rise Black Dragon Ship would obscure his view, it seemed as if he intended to convey the emotion he was speaking of clearly.

The moment his gaze reached Plum Blossom Island, several members of Shaolin flinched and trembled. They still could not look directly in that direction.

Jang Ilso turned his gaze back to Shaolin and nodded as if amazed.

“Life is precious to everyone. But these people were willing to give up their own lives for the sake of agreement and honor!”

A soft, snickering laugh flowed down the river.

It was truly incomprehensible. Since it was Jang Ilso who spoke those words, they should have sounded mocking, but there was clearly admiration in his tone. So Shaolin, who was listening, couldn’t help but feel confused.

“Namgung proved it. The agreement they were crying about was not just words.”

He seemed to understand what it meant to be drawn into words. Jang Ilso’s every word captivated them.

However...

“Shut up!”

It seemed the abbot could not simply listen to Jang Ilso’s words. He interrupted with a loud roar. However, there was a sense of urgency mixed with the roar that should have been majestic.

It seemed like if they didn’t silence Jang Ilso right now, something significant would happen.

“You are the wicked devil! Shaolin disciples should not be deceived by such words. Keep your minds strong!”

At those words, the Shaolin disciples looked at the abbot.

Strong mind? What on earth did Jang Ilso mean by saying he was deceiving them?

“I guess you don’t understand.”

“You...”

“I told you. I am just trying to give you a chance.”

As Jang Ilso laughed softly, Beop Gye’s face contorted horribly, standing next to the abbot. That disgusting smile brought back memories of that day three years ago.

“What kind of chance are you giving us?”

“Beop Gye!”

The abbot desperately tried to silence Beop Gye, but there was no way to take back what had been said.

And then, as if waiting for those words to come out, Jang Ilso’s red lips twisted.

“Your principles... a chance to prove it.”

The abbot’s eyes turned red.

“This...”

As if he could not hide his anger, the veins stood out on his neck.

However, Jang Ilso laughed as if he was delighted to see him.

“Hahahaha! Why are you so angry, great monk?”

Then he spread his arms wide and pointed behind him with an exaggerated gesture.

“There! They are there.”

“...”

“Eradicate the evil demon!”

The hem of his clothes fluttered as if dancing.

“Save the people! Those who risked their lives to protect that principle are right there! They are right before your eyes!”

His exaggerated tone, as if he was performing a singing act, was awkward, but that was also why they could not take their eyes off him. When his arms slowly lowered, the Shaolin monks also held their breath.

Jang Ilso slowly began to bring down the atmosphere he had raised all at once. His eyebrows softened as if he was sad.

“But unfortunately... they are dying now.”

A deep sigh escaped his red lips.

“Those who are seriously injured won’t be able to hold out for long. They will be surrounded by a group of evil people, exhausted, and will follow them one by one. No matter how great the warriors are, if they are isolated on an island without a single grain, the result will be obvious.”

The abbot tightly gripped the hem of his robe. The fabric turned to dust due to the control he was trying to maintain, but the abbot was too busy glaring at Jang Ilso, as if he wanted to kill him, to realize it.

“But fortunately....”

At that moment, Jang Ilso’s gaze scanned everyone in Shaolin.

“There are heroes here who can save them.”

Snap!

The sound of Jang Ilso snapping his fingers instantly brightened the atmosphere.

“It isn’t so difficult. Yes, it isn’t difficult.”

“....”

“Saving those in danger is something that a righteous martial artist should do, right? Right?”

Everyone in Shaolin bit their lips tightly. Only then did they understand what Jang Ilso was trying to say.

“Of course, of course! There are obstacles. Those wicked Evil Sect people are blocking the way! But... that shouldn’t be such a problem, right? The Namgung family proved their motto with their life, so you will not say that the world-renowned Shaolin cannot do that, right?”

“Jang Ils....”

Snap.

Before the abbot could even speak, Jang Ilso snapped his fingers again.

“Giving you a chance.”

The abbot wanted to block his ears.

That devil was digging into the hearts of the people. He seemed to instinctively know what the weakest part of a human heart was. He seemed to dig into the weakest spot, pick at the wound, sprinkle salt on it, and trample it with his feet.

“When the sun rises on the 5th day from now, we will step onto the island again.”

A slightly excited voice flowed from Jang Ilso’s mouth.

“At that time, everyone will definitely die... not a single one will be left alive. Everyone with the name of Namgung.”

The abbot’s fingers trembled.

“So, before five days pass, you must decide. Are you going to watch them die by our hands, or are you going to fight us and open the path to save them?”

“Jang Ilsoooo!”

A loud roar erupted from the abbot’s mouth once again.

“How can a human do that? How can someone with the skin of a human...”

That was clearly justified anger.

Leaving the abbot’s actions aside, taking hundreds of people’s lives hostage, and dragging another victim into the ant pit was clearly something no human could do.

However, Jang Ilso tilted his head as if he couldn’t understand the abbot’s anger.

“Are you... talking to me, great monk?”

“You wicked bastard! If not you, who will I speak to?”

“Ah, seriously...”

Jang Ilso laughed.

“I thought you were talking to yourself, great monk.”

The abbot bit his lips until they bled.

“Right? You were watching. You saw them being torn apart by the pirates and trampled under my own feet with your eyes.”

Jang Ilso’s eyes glared at everyone in Shaolin.

It was truly strange. Jang Ilso was the leader of the Evil Sect, and those standing here were all the vessels of the Justice faction who protect the world. However, no one standing there could look Jang Ilso in the eye.

It wasn’t Jang Ilso who turned their gaze. It was the conscience that existed in their hearts. Jang Ilso had touched that conscience.

“How can you leave them alone, wearing the masks of humans? Uh? Of all places, Shaolin, the sect that protects the people of Central Plains? Oh my....”

“YOUUUUU!”

The abbot burst into anger. If the distance had been a bit closer, he would have killed Jang Ilso in a single blow.

Unfortunately, there was a river flowing between them, and all the abbot could do was vent his anger without any use.

“Ahahahahah!”

Jang Ilso laughed like a child as if he was so happy with the abbot’s reaction.

“Why are you so angry, great monk? Shouldn’t you be happy instead? No matter how much you harbor a grudge in your heart, if you don’t get a chance to prove it, you cannot be a righteous person, right?”

Those words seemed to ask the opposite.

He had been speaking so well about maintaining justice and peace, but could he really risk his life for that cause? Didn’t he hesitate once because he was worried about his life?

He wanted to close his eyes.

He wanted to cover his ears.

Everyone in Shaolin realized that no matter how much they changed their actions from now on, the act of stopping while watching Namgung in a desperate situation just a moment ago would not disappear.

They had already lost the peace and harmony they had been discussing so much.

Why did they stop? Because the abbot stopped them?

That could not be.

Actually, they were afraid. Giving up one’s life for someone else had seemed like a natural act, but the moment they had their first chance to do so, what held them back was the desire not to die.

The abbot merely gave them an excuse. An excuse to stop and shift the blame to someone else.

“A-amitabha.”

“Amitabha.”

Chants leaked out from everywhere, indicating that their hearts were wavering.

Jang Ilso took the truth they wanted to ignore and engraved it right into their flesh, bones, and hearts.

“So, prove it to your heart’s content.”

Jang Ilso’s lips parted, revealing white teeth.

“Are you really the kind of person who would risk everything for an agreement like you always say?”

“Eik....”

“Or are you just hypocrites who took advantage of the word ‘righteous’?”

Jang Ilso briefly tapped his lips with his fingernails.

“That... with such a great life.”

“....”

“If it’s Jang Ilso and the Four Precepts Alliance, they will be worthy opponents.”

Jang Ilso made the final move.

“Do not forget. Five days. That should be enough time to think about this. Be deeply grateful for my mercy. Great monk! Ahahahaha!”

After finishing those words, he turned around, waving the red robe as if he didn’t care about Shaolin. He didn’t show a speck of regret.

Until his figure slowly disappeared into the Black Dragon Ship, no one could catch him.

No one could prove their will against him.

The abbot gritted his teeth and stared at the pitiful river with red eyes.

“... Amitabha.”

A frustrated chant escaped the abbot’s mouth.

The Black Dragon Ship blocked their path alongside the tightly packed Four Sects Alliance’s fleet. And the Plum Blossom Island. The abbot, who had been observing the Namgung family’s visible swordsmen around, closed his eyes.

“Amitabha....”

It would have been better not to have listened. He should have known that it was already too late from the moment the devil began to whisper into their ears.

It was too frightening to look back. It was hard to see what kind of expressions the disciples standing behind him would have.

‘This...’

The abbot covered his face with one hand, completely losing it.

‘This place is hell.’

Where the devil stood, that place soon turned into hell.

Ho Ga-Myung, who was standing on the deck, greeted Jang Ilso politely.

“Thank you for your hard work, Alliance Lord.”

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso slowly nodded.

However, unlike Ho Ga-Myung, the Black Dragon King’s complexion wasn’t so bright.

“Jang Ilso... no, Alliance lord.”

Black Dragon King asked with a stern face.

“I don’t understand what the Alliance Lord is doing. Is it really necessary to keep Namgung alive for 5 days?”

“...”

“I understand that it is needed to clearly inform them that Namgung will be crushed. However, there is no need for us to give them time...”

Jang Ilso slowly turned to him.

“It is needed.”

“....”

“It is needed, Black Dragon King.”

Jang Ilso’s lips twisted into a sneer.

“If we kill all of those of the Namgung family right now, what will Shaolin say to the other sects who arrived after that?”

“... that.”

The Black Dragon King closed his mouth as he was about to say something.

Would Shaolin simply confess that they couldn’t jump into the river because they faced Jang Ilso?

‘They would never do that.’

If it was him over there, he would never have said that. He would say that he ran as fast as he could, but when he got there, Namgung had already been defeated by the Evil Sect.

Right now, no one in the Yangtze River could refute Shaolin’s words. All that was left were Namgung, Four Precepts Alliance, and Shaolin.

Even if the curious commoners who wandered around the Yangtze River saw this, who would believe them? The people there right now were Shaolin, the leader of murim.

“We can deal with Namgung at any time. The important thing is to let the world know that Shaolin stopped there and just watched Namgung’s collapse.”

Jang Ilso laughed coldly.

“Our enemy is not Namgung, but the Nine Great Sects.”

“....”

The Black Dragon King felt a chill run down his spine.

The pirates took their time and skillfully manipulated the Namgung family. However, he had no intention of using the Namgung family as bait to humiliate Shaolin and twist the Justice faction.

Who would think of such a thing?

The Black Dragon King was also curious. What kind of expression would Shaolin have when the other sects arrived? Perhaps a face mixed with guilt and humiliation.

“... But the arrival of support would mean that their power has grown stronger. What if they really try to cross the river to save Namgung?”

“They?”

Jang Ilso snorted.

“If they were like that, the war would have already started.”

“....”

“Once they stop, they cannot get back up. If time was pressing, they could have attacked as if they were being pushed back. But...”

Jang Ilso turned his head and looked towards Shaolin. Then he asked softly.

“Can you see?”

“...”

“What do you think they are thinking right now? Do you think they are happy to have the chance to save Namgung?”

The Black Dragon King frowned.

“If it was me....”

The white teeth appeared between Jang Ilso’s bright red lips. It was as creepy as looking at a beast with fangs exposed.

“I would rather have all of those Namgung ones die without any exception and close their mouths before anything comes out.”

The Black Dragon King looked at Jang Ilso with a blank expression.

“Even if they get support and save Namgung, it won’t come back good for Shaolin. The credit will go to those who arrive later. Even if Shaolin fights fiercely, the only thing they will gain is the Namgung family, who harbors a terrible grudge against them and returns to the Northern Lands.”

“....”

“I will ask again....”

Cold sweat ran down the Black Dragon King’s back.

“Do you really think Shaolin wants to save Namgung? Really?”

“...”

“I... don’t think so.”

Jang Ilso chuckled softly as if he was going insane because this situation was fun to him.

The Black Dragon King felt a chill and chewed the inside of his lip.

‘Insane bastard.’

This man was out of his mind.

Control?

There was no way the Black Dragon King could control this man. He and the other Four Precepts Alliance’s sect leaders were clearly mistaken about something.

This man would definitely hold the world in his hands and shake it up as he pleased.

“Humans, you see...”

At that moment, Jang Ilso stopped laughing and looked at the Black Dragon King with a calm expression. His eyes seemed no different from theirs.

“Consider words and actions as different.”

“....”

“People’s words are nothing more than things they have embellished for their own benefits. Their true feelings are always hidden deep inside. And the funny thing is...”

Jang Ilso, who had been speaking softly, turned to Shaolin.

“Surprisingly, people are the ones who don’t know their true feelings very well.”

“....”

“Aren’t you curious? How will that abbot react? And how will those bastards who have been taught to follow the abbot’s orders their entire lives react?”

Jang Ilso slowly touched his lips with his fingertips.

“What is certain is that those who have seen this true and ugly nature will never return to their original form. Be it Shaolin or Namgung, killing a few people isn’t that important. What is important is letting them know they are not that great of people.”

“....”

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso’s long eyes slightly looked down.

“There is no alcohol here.”

The Black Dragon King quickly nodded. Those who had been overwhelmed by Jang Ilso’s momentum were startled and ran to the cabin.

Even though they went to get the alcohol, Jang Ilso showed no sign of going in, so the Black Dragon King asked.

“You will have it here?”

“Isn’t this a nice place?”

Jang Ilso chuckled.

“It would be pretty cool to drink alcohol while watching Shaolin, who cannot do a thing.”

The Black Dragon King nodded heavily.

“I-I will bring the table here...”

The man, who brought the alcohol, had a pale face and was even stammering his words. Then Jang Ilso slowly held out his hand.

“Start with the alcohol.”

“Uh? Ah... yes!”

The man politely handed over the bottle. Jang Ilso slowly walked to the railing with the bottle in hand and sat down.

“This is nice.”

His gaze turned to Shaolin across the river. It was quite a sight to see them unable to take a single step from there as if they had turned into stone.

A person with conviction will jump into a pit of fire. However, a person who stops in front of a pit of fire can never muster the courage to jump in.

What Jang Ilso wanted was to leave a deep mark on their hearts.

‘One victory is good, after all.’

The important thing was to win in the end.

At that moment, Ho Ga-Myung approached him cautiously.

“Alliance lord.”

“Uh?”

“Of course, they will never be able to jump into the river. But the sects that arrive later might be different from Shaolin, right?”

“Even so, they are all just pigs. Shaolin will not step out, so do you think they dare to fight us alone?”

“....”

“Namgung, who should be leading the Five Great Families, is like that, and the Tang family has broken away. Shaolin doesn’t even know what it is doing, and Wudang, which should be their alternative, has sealed itself behind its gates.”

Jang Ilso, who was slowly reciting the situation, raised his lips into a smile.

“All that is left are just those idiots with loud voices.”

Ho Ga-Myung slowly nodded.

“I understand.”

Jang Ilso, who thought he had finished speaking, opened his mouth again.

“However...”

“Uh?”

Ho Ga-Myung looked at him with a puzzled expression, but Jang Ilso didn’t answer him right away and just smiled oddly.

“There is one over there too.”

“... What do you mean?”

“The unpredictable insane guy.”

“....”

“Hahahaha.”

Jang Ilso shook his head happily and started drinking from the bottle.

‘Right?’

His gaze was fixed on the north.

Namgung Do-Wei collapsed on the spot.

Everyone heard it. Everyone.

Jang Ilso proudly poured his internal qi into his voice to make everyone in the river hear it as if he wanted to ensure everyone knew of the situation.

Thanks to that, everyone on Plum Blossom Island had to hear the conversation between Jang Ilso and the abbot.

“Haha....”

A hollow laugh escaped Namgung Do-Wei’s mouth.

Was it really so cruel?

The world of murim and the passion he had dreamed of was nothing more than an illusion. The reality of it was terribly cruel.

But what made it even more painful was the fact that he wasn’t even given the luxury of looking away from all this.

“Ughh...”

“Cough.”

The sounds filled with groans could be heard from around. Namgung swordsmen were bleeding and writhing in pain. Those who had been holding on to their mental strength despite the injuries had collapsed as if their mind and body had given up.

There were even quite a few who were in such critical condition that they wouldn’t be able to survive the day if things continued this way.

‘What did we come here for?’

When they advanced to Plum Blossom Island to protect their pride as a sect and the Justice faction, they didn’t hesitate an inch. However, now that the situation had come to such a point, they couldn’t help but laugh at themselves.

Did they really exist? Did the sect and the Justice sect’s pride really exist?

Then why did Shaolin stop there and just let Jang Ilso play around with them?

“Ha... haha....”

Everything felt like it was being denied. Everything about him...

Tak.

But at that moment, someone put a hand on Namgung Do-Wei’s shoulder.

“....”

He looked back and saw Namgung Hwang grabbing his shoulder.

His face was pale as if the psychological blow hadn’t gone away, but his lips looked firmer than ever.

“Get up.”

“... father.”

“Collect the injured. This is a fight to hold on. We have to save at least one more person.”

“....”

“For the next five days...”

“And if we do hold on?”

“...”

Namgung Do-Wei, so angry he couldn’t speak, bit his lips until they bled.

“What about after holding on for those five days? Since we have endured it well, can we just die in satisfaction?”

“Do-Wei....”

“Do you think they will help us, lord? For real?”

Namgung Hwang closed his eyes.

His son lived up to his name until the last moment, to the point that even Namgung Hwang was surprised.

So his sense of betrayal must have been the greatest in this time, more than anyone else around.

“Those people....”

Clench.

Just as Namgung Do-Wei was about to speak again, Namgung Hwang grabbed his shoulder tightly.

“Just because they will not help you, will you let our comrades die?”

“....”

“There is no such thing as meaningless resistance. Sometimes, you just have to do what you have to do rather than accomplish something.”

Namgung Do-Wei bit down on his lip.

His shoulders trembled. Namgung Hwang waited for Namgung Do-Wei in silence.

“I am sorry. I was....”

“Enough.”

Namgung Do-Wei struggled to raise his heavy body.

“I will try to collect the injured and endure this as long as I can, but...”

“I know.”

In this place where there wasn’t a single grain of rice left, there must be a limit to how long the injured can hold out. There is no way to know how many will survive during those five days.

“As long as they survive.”

“....”

“Even if it’s just one person.”

Namgung Do-Wei slowly closed his eyes while listening to Namgung Hwang. When he took a deep breath and opened his eyes, they were filled with firmness.

He immediately turned around and shouted.

“Those who can move, follow me! I will collect and treat the wounded!”

“... Yes!”

The response that came back lacked any liveliness, clearly reflecting the predicament Namgung was facing.

However, Namgung Do-Wei didn’t push them to be in high spirits. Instead, he quietly concentrated on tending to the wounded. There were clearly times when talking wouldn’t improve the situation, no matter how much he tried.

Namgung Hwang gradually shifted his gaze to Namgung Do-Wei and Namgung Myung, who were working diligently.

The ships encircled the island.

And Shaolin had halted beyond the island.

‘So lonely.’

It felt as if the world had abandoned them.

“Mother, my legs hurt.”

“Just a little more. Just endure this.”

“But my legs hurt....”

The woman carrying the baggage patted her child on his back while he was begging her to carry him. She must have wanted to carry her child right away, but the bundle she was carrying wasn’t that small.

No, it was too heavy and big for an average woman to carry.

“Damn it.”

The man, who seemed to be the child’s father, looked back with a face of resentment and anger.

“What is happening to this world....”

They were just leaving the Yangtze River.

They had lived their entire lives cultivating the lands and casting nets alongside the river, but they couldn’t stay there anymore in the face of the war and the tension around the Yangtze River.

Didn’t they all have only one life?

“It will take three days before we see anything...”

The man sighed deeply as he watched his wife rubbing the child’s legs.

‘Even if we decided to move....’

It wasn’t easy for those who had lived in one place all their lives to settle down in a new location. But they couldn’t continue staying there.

There was no guarantee that a new war would not break out even if the current war ended. As a result, those who bravely survived the chaos of three years ago on the Yangtze River eventually had no choice but to leave their homes and go on the road when they saw the war.

“Let us find a suitable place and rest.”

“... okay.”

Just as the man was about to sigh again.

“Hmm?”

The man frowned after spotting something. He stared at it, and his eyes widened.

“W-what is that?”

“Which one?”

“T-that thing!”

The man raised his hand and pointed to one side. Something white was rushing in.

“Is that a sandstorm?”

“What is a sandstorm doing with no wind... doesn’t it seem to be getting bigger?”

“Now that you say...”

The man’s mouth opened wider and wider.

The white dust that rose from the end of the road they had walked gradually grew in size and soon became a huge dust cloud, fiercely approaching them.

“G-get to the side of the road!”

The man held the child in his arms, shocked. If the ones causing the dust were from the Evil Sect who had crossed the Yangtze River, then wouldn’t their lives be in danger?

“Over there!”

The three of them, frightened, hurriedly stepped aside.

‘What should we do? Should we run from here...’
